2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 澳洲 8 月就業人數勁增 3.95 萬人大幅優於預期，但勞動參與率上升至 67.1%，
推升失業率至 4.6%，呈現勞動力擴增但市場邊際降溫跡象。澳洲央行總裁布拉
克日前指出，失業率回升至5%有助於抑制頑固通膨。考量物價長期高於目標中
點，市場預期澳洲央行本週二將重啟緊縮，升息 1 碼至 4.60%，創 2011年底以
來新高。
 瑞士央行（SNB）上週四宣布連續第五次將關鍵政策利率維持在0%不變，符合
市場預期。儘管受油價推升使 8 月通膨微升至 0.8%，但仍處於目標區間，央行
預期中期通膨壓力溫和，現行極度寬鬆立場依然適當。決議公布後瑞郎兌美元跌
至 1.2077。相較全球主要央行相繼緊縮，瑞士央行維持超低利率，策略轉為視情
況調節匯市以穩定瑞郎。
 標普全球公布歐元區 9 月綜合 PMI 初值升至 53.1，創三年新高，製造與服務業
同步擴張，新訂單增速達四年多最快，印證經濟無懼美伊衝突引發的能源成本飆

升，展現超預期韌性。受能源價格傳導加速影響，通膨壓力再度升溫，儘管尚未
浮現薪資二輪效應，但強勁景氣數據促使市場大幅提高歐洲央行提前於10月例
會再度升息的押注。
 美國 9 月綜合 PMI 初值勁升至 58.4，創 62 個月新高；製造業與服務業 PMI 亦
逆勢走強至 57與 58.7，凸顯企業擴張強勁，引發市場對通膨持續性的擔憂。市
場焦點轉向 9 月 30日公布的美國 8 月 PCE、個人消費支出與 Q2 GDP終值，同
日日本亦將發布短觀調查與央行意見摘要。若終端消費延續強勁，將牽動美日央
行政策利率預期。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.655，低點在 31.866，以 31.790作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 2.8分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，台股創新高，外資連續五日買超台股並匯入，推升
台幣最高來到 31.655，不過逢低吸引進口商、政府基金等買盤進場，台幣主要在
31.700 附近震盪；臨近周末，美國 9 月綜合 PMI 由 8 月的 56 走升至 58.4，創下逾
5 年來的新高，加上美伊戰事持續，皆推升市場對後續升息的預期，美元指數升至
101 上方，台幣跳空開低，回貶至 31.800 上方，最低來到 31.866，不過隨後出口商
賣壓出籠，台幣跌勢放緩，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.790。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國經濟表現仍具韌性，9月綜合
PMI 由 8 月的 56 上升至 58.4，企業活動加速至逾 5 年來最強，數據公布後美國 10
年債券殖利率升破 5%，Fed Watch 顯示市場對於聯準會 10 月升息預期升高，有助
於美元指數維持偏強格局，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，台股在創下
新高後回落，而外資也結束連續五日買超的走勢，轉為賣超，短線上資金匯入不利
台幣表現，然而，台灣8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6億美元、年增 71.4%，創歷史新高，
AI 需求帶動出口持續強勁，出口商後續拋匯需求預計將提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，
預估本週台幣區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走貶。人民幣中間價在上周連續十天走升，並升破6.7500整數
關口，最高來到 6.7468，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美伊戰事持續，市場對於聯準
會升息預期提升，美元指數震盪走強，加上週間公布美國9月綜合 PMI由 8 月的 56
走升至 58.4，創下逾 5 年來的新高，美元指數突破 101 整數關卡，令境外人民幣承
壓，USD/CNH回貶至 6.7200上方，終場境外人民幣收在6.7227，週線貶值283點，
境內人民幣則收在 6.7125，週線貶值 141 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣高檔震盪。就國際市場來看，美中兩國完成新一輪談判，美方
表示雙方同意將原訂 11 月 10 日到期的貿易休戰延長至明年 1 月 10 日，並持續就
關稅、貿易及供應鏈等議題進行協商，降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險，對人民幣形
成一定支撐。不過，美國近期經濟數據仍具韌性，加以聯準會偏鷹立場推升美元，
預計將限制人民幣升值空間；就中國國內市場來看，近期人行持續偏強設定中間價，
帶動人民幣維持偏強走勢，惟美中利差再度擴大，預計將限制人民幣升值的空間；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，匯價反彈至 20日均線之上，顯示原先下
行動能暫歇，上方壓力先看向季線 6.7350附近，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣高檔震
盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7400

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY先上後下。週初，美國多位聯準會官員鷹派談話，帶動美元指數
持續上行，推升 USD/JPY延續前一週的走勢來到 157上方；週間，受美國 PMI 強
於預期與美債殖利率全面上行帶動，市場重新提高對聯準會後續升息路徑的押注，
帶動 USD/JPY大漲突破 159的關卡；臨近週末，日本首相高市早苗表態日圓被低估
是個問題，使市場提高對政策訊號與潛在干預的敏感度，帶動USD/JPY回吐大部分
漲幅，終場收在 157.26，週線上漲 56點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本財務大臣片山皋月上週
五罕見透露，美國總統川普在美日元首峰會上對日圓過度疲弱表達關切，首相高市
早苗亦回應日圓遭低估確屬問題。儘管近期美元指數維持在 101 上方，但在日本政
府可能干預的陰影下，預計將限縮日圓貶值的空間。二、美國9 月綜合 PMI產出指
數創逾五年新高，疊加美伊戰事推升油價突破100美元與柴油大漲，通膨壓力劇增。
美聯儲理事巴爾表態可能需再升息兩次，芝加哥聯儲古爾斯比也示警能源衝擊具持
續性，推升市場押注 10 月升息機率升至六成，預計將支撐 USD/JPY 走勢。三、就
技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY連日上漲一度站回年線但隨即失守，目前依舊承
壓於季線及年線下方，不過各天期均線趨平，且下方尚有月線支撐，短線尚無明確
的方向。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.30-159.30 
前週交易區間 
156.56 159.03

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，歐元延續前一週偏弱的走勢，加上聯準會官員鷹
派發言，導致歐元跌破 1.1450的重要關卡；週間，儘管油價自高檔回落，但市場解
讀為能源成本仍處高位、難以迅速回落，推升對美國實質利率與利差優勢的預期。
加上歐洲在能源依賴與成長動能較弱下，相對於美國更難承受長期高利率環境，歐
元最低來到 1.1358；臨近週末，歐元跌深反彈，終場收在1.1391，週線下跌 91點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學家連
恩示警，第二波能源價格飆升將推升通膨，預計至2027年中期始回落至目標水準。
德國央行月報指出，受中東衝突、低天然氣庫存及明年醫療改革影響，通膨將居高
不下；夏季雖因出口疲弱與乾旱致增長趨緩，但在製造業回暖與財政支出挹注下，
估年底經濟有望重啟復甦，預計將提供歐元一定的支撐。二、美聯儲官員接連釋放
強烈鷹派信號，聖路易聯儲總裁穆薩萊姆直言當前 3.75%-4.00%利率仍偏寬鬆，呼
籲盡早收緊；芝加哥聯儲總裁古爾斯比亦警告，AI投資繁榮引發需求過熱，疊加持
續性供應衝擊，可能迫使美聯儲加速且更積極地升息。目前Fedwatch顯示預期明年
4 月前可能再升息三次，將不利歐元表現。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
在各天期均線下方，且接連失守1.15及 1.14整數關卡，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1300-1.1450 
前週交易區間 
1.1358 1.1495

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.21-115.09.24 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.804% ~ 0.830%  
30天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.69%  
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95%  
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.84%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本周央行持續每日減發定存單，令市場資金充裕，帶動短
率靠向區間低位。 
週二央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.638%，略低於
市場預期，且較上次標售結果下跌 2.5個基本點。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.804%~0.830%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.69%，利率較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 及 5 年期指標券 A15108 均未成
交，週四等殖系統參考報價中價分別為 1.95%、1.84%。 
台股排擠效應、國際債市走勢偏空，加上油價續處高檔，通膨潛在威脅仍存，
12 月升息可能性未能完全排除，市場氣氛續偏觀望，預期台債殖利率將維持
高檔區間盤整 ， 預估5年券區間為 1.84%~1.90%，10年券區間為 1.95%~2.0%。

### Page 1

Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co.

3Q26 earnings preview: steeper 2nm ramp 
lifting sales upside at resilient margins

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 3,260 TWD | Price: 2,550 TWD

Solid HPC and flagship mobile pipeline solidifies 2H26 
TSMC’s 3Q26 sales were above street +18% QoQ in NT$ and reached US$46.7bn (vs. its 
guide of US$44.6-45.8bn) which we believe should be driven by the builds for Apple’s 
mobile SoC and AMD’s server CPU on 2nm along with multiple GPU and ASIC product 
refreshes migrating to 3nm. The mix upgrades could lift 4Q26 sales by +10-15% QoQ, 
putting it on track to the guidance for 40%+ YoY in ’26. While utilization and pricing are 
favorable, GMs could hold steady in 3Q/4Q26 (BofAe 67.0%/67.8%) rather than expand 
(recent street 67-68%/68-69%) factoring dilution from 2nm and overseas fab ramps. 
Tight supply enables greater value capture and investment 
On solid AI demand outlook, TSMC’s capex should be on track to its lifted guidance of 
US$60-64bn in ’26 (BofAe US$63bn). The investment could see another surge in ’27 
(BofAe US$83bn) for further N3, N2, A16 and CoWoS-L expansion and new technologies 
ramp (e.g. CoPoS and CPO). The tool makers’ modest price hike could require TSMC to 
spend 10% more capex though should be reflected in its value when the capacity starts 
production in ’28 on top of a 5-10% leading edge ASP lift in 1Q27 on supply tightness. 
Checkpoints: AI demand, capex & competition/partnership 
1) TSMC’s view on cloud AI investment amid rate hike + challenging FCF, 2) comment on 
the market and its growth and capex commitment, 3) update on the competition and 
collaboration (see Exhibit 29 for our analysis on Terafab/SpaceX) and US investment.  
AI-led earnings outlook firmer; valuation should catch up 
We lift our 26/27/28 EPS by 2-9% to NT$112/NT$161/NT$203 and keep our Buy rating 
on TSMC and raise PO to NT$3,260/US$620 on 20x ‘27E P/E. Its current valuation is 
undemanding at 16x ‘27E P/E (vs. its long term 11-21x) backed by its advanced 
technology leadership.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
TSMC was founded in 1987 and is listed in Taiwan 
(2330TT) and the US (TSM/NYS).TSMC is the largest and 
global leader in integrated circuit (IC) manufacturing. As a 
build-to-order foundry, it provides a wide range of value-
add activities: IC manufacturing, mask-making, IC design 
services, turnkey solutions, and process development. We 
attribute its success to its proven, winning business model, 
unparalleled scale advantage, optimized execution, and 
technology scope and depth. 5 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
The ongoing semi content growth in mobile, rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI), and proliferation of Internet of Things (IoT) 
should result in sustainable upside in aggregate computing 
power globally. TSMC, as the leading contract manufacturer 
of semiconductor chips, is in a good position to capitalize. 
We are structurally positive on TSMC and expect it to 
sustain over 15% growth with rising FCF from 2026 
onwards.

### Page 4

3Q26 sales topped guidance; GMs tracking
well but recent street could be too bullish
We profile our expectation for TSMC’s 3Q26 earnings on October 15th. 3Q26 sales were
reported +18% QoQ in NT$, above BofAe/street expectations of +14%/+13% QoQ.
Based on the average FX of NT$31.99/US$ (vs. its NT$32US$ guide), its sales in US$
term would be US$46.7bn, also above its guidance range of US$44.6-45.8bn. we believe
the strength is led by strong ramp on 2nm potentially growing to 11% of its sales in

3Q26 (vs. 3% in 2Q26) for Apple’s builds for its product launches and surging demand
for AMD’s Venice server CPU. These growth drivers on the node are supplemented by
the incremental inventory stocking from Mediatek and Qualcomm for their flagship
smartphone SoCs albeit at a low base and below seasonal due to weak Android demand.
On top of the 2nm scale up, we expect the utilization for 5nm and 3nm to stay above
100% backed by the continued HPC/AI build strength especially with multiple product

upgrades including 1) accelerators (GPUs and ASICs): strong demand for Nvidia’s Rubin
GPU, AMD’s MI400, AWS’s Trainium3 ramp and initial ramp for Google’s TPUv8t/i more
than offsetting a mild wind down in Google’s Ironwood TPU, 2) CPUs: solid demand for
general purpose servers and AI data centers as noted in our deep dive report quantifying
CPU demand through ’30 across x86 (AMD’s Turin CPUs) and ARM based CPUs (e.g.
Nvidia’s Vera CPU, Google’s Axion CPU, AWS Graviton CPU along with incremental

contribution from Microsoft Cobalt CPU) and 3) networking switch: more and faster
data transmission in the complex AI data centers requires more advanced  networking
switch solutions from Nvidia/Mellanox, Broadcom and Marvell.
The company’s better than expected sales in 3Q26 backed by healthy shipment and
pricing trend on the advanced nodes should be supportive to its GMs. However, in
contrast to the recent aggressive street expectations of 67-68% GMs in 3Q26, we only

model it at the high-end of its guidance of 65-67%, balancing more favorable utilization
and gradual 3nm depreciation roll off partially offset by a 3-4% GMs dilution from 2nm
ramp which we believe is driver for the incremental sales during the quarter.

### Page 5

HPC/Apple lifting 4Q26 sales outlook;
resilient GM expectation but below street
For 4Q26, based on its slightly above +40% YoY full year sales guide, TSMC’s sales could
grow 10-15% QoQ (BofAe +11%), better than its 10-year seasonality of 7% QoQ even
off a high base from 1Q-3Q26. The blended ASPs strength (BofAe +11% QoQ) should be
the key driver for 4Q26 sales as shipment mix continues to shift toward advanced nodes,
with revenue from 5nm and below processes representing 75% of the company’s sales

(vs. 66%/71% in 2Q/3Q26). Specifically, 2nm with 15%+ higher wafer pricing than 3nm
could further grow its contribution to 18% of TSMC’s sales during the quarter. On the
other hand, we model a stable wafer shipment (BofAe +9%/-5% QoQ for
advanced/mature nodes), balancing HPC demand strength and Apple’s seasonal chipset
builds for its new product launches and a modest drop on the specialty process output
and effective capacity loss due to equipment movement for the node conversion.

While the blended pricing is even more favorable and utilization on the advanced and
mature nodes in general stay healthy, we model a relatively stable GMs only up to
~67.8% levels in 4Q26 (vs. recent street and investors’ expectations at 68-69%). The key
factors we consider as mildly negative offset would be 1) higher depreciation: +25-30%
HoH in 2H26 due to more overseas and 2nm capacity ramp and 2) 2nm still going
through yield learning curve and could see more meaningful impact in 4Q26 (vs. its 3-4%

GMs dilution guide in 2H26) as the sales contribution grows.

### Page 6

2026-27 sales and margins outlook 
solidified by healthy AI demand 
Factoring in stronger CPU and AI GPU/ASIC demand outlook as noted in our Semicon 
Taiwan takeaways report where we updated our industry models, we are lifting our US$ 
sales expectation for TSMC in ‘26/’27 to +42%/+40% YoY respectively (vs. BofA prior 
+41%/+40% YoY). More importantly, with utilization staying tighter for longer along with 
inflationary cost pass through (e.g. equipment and consumables), TSMC should raise its 
advanced node and packaging pricing by 5-10% in the beginning of 2027 to reflect its 
value. This along with a mild 3-5% price hike on the mature nodes where the utilization 
is back to 90-95% as it optimizes the product mix following the strategic 6”/8” capacity 
retirement should lift its GMs to 67%/69% in 2026/27 (vs. BofA prior 67%/67%).

Capex on track for lifted guide for 2026 
and will grow 20%+ CAGR from ’26-28 
We lifted our front-end foundry industry capex growth expectation to 19%/26% YoY for 
‘26/27 to US$86bn/US$108bn, supporting a CAGR of 16% from ’26-28. This is mostly 
led by our higher expectation on TSMC’s capex on stronger wafer demand on N3 and N2 
from GPUs, ASICs, CPUs, networking switches and HBM base dies and initial ramp of 
A16, along with incremental spending reacceleration from tier 2 foundries. 
Separately, we also raised the back-end packaging and testing industry spending 
estimates from 66%/36% YoY to 82%/21% YoY for ‘26/27 to US$27bn/US$33bn, 
supporting a CAGR of 15% from ’26-28. The upward revision is driven by our higher 
expectation of TSMC’s more aggressive capacity addition on CoWoS-R/L to 
accommodate more demand from its customers amid growing level of chipset 
complexity and reticle size and higher level of capacity readiness of SoIC for higher 3D 
packaging adoption for logic and memory dies integration and CPO. The more 
aggressive spending from ASE is set to grow by another 30-50% YoY in 2027 off a high 
base in 2026 on its own FoCoS capacity ramp and TSMC’s CoWoS licensing.

Exhibit 12: Back-end sales, capex, and capital intensity 
Back-end capex should reach US$27bn/US$33bn in ‘26/27

Source: Company Data, BofA Global Research estimates, Bloomberg 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Specifically on TSMC, based on our bottom up capacity analysis across front-end 
manufacturing and back-end packaging, TSMC’s capex spending is tracking to US$63bn 
for 2026 (vs. its latest guidance of US$60-64bn) mostly skewed toward advanced front-
end manufacturing including 55-60k WPM 3nm capacity addition, 60k WPM 2nm 
capacity ramp, initial 3-5k WPM A16 capacity installation. The back-end packaging 
investment should be largely driven by its CoWoS capacity growth while the spending on 
the equipment pull-in for CPO and CoPoS remains limited as most of the equipment are 
still under sampling process for qualification and meaningful shipment will be in 2027-
28. 
In addition to the earlier tool pull-in schedule due to tighter equipment supply with 
memory makers expanding, the fast-growing capex should reflect the rising cost per 1k 
WPM on the advanced nodes vs. mature nodes, with the equipment cost per 1k WPM 
advanced wafer capacity expansion has grown from US$156mn in 2023 to US$241mn in 
2025. Even factoring in more common use of equipment on the advanced nodes, the 
capex/1k WPM advanced capacity could further rise by 20% to US$289mn in 2027 with 
the technology architecture upgrade. 
On top of the investment in the core front-end manufacturing, we expect the company 
to slightly outspend in the back-end packaging and estimate it is at the upper half of its 
guidance for 10-20% total capex, mainly for more aggressive capacity expansion to 
meet its customers’ demand amid growing competition from Intel’s EMIB-T solution and 
technology innovation. On the other hand, the maintenance capex could be reduced for 
specialty technologies as the company gradually retires the equipment.

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
Considering the capex required for further 3nm capacity expansion in the additional phases in 
its Tainan megafab, Arizona and the recently announced 2nd fab in Kumamoto on strong 
AI/HPC/automotive demand, ramping scale for 2nm family including A16, R&D on A14  
supplemented by multiple back-end packaging technologies in progress (e.g. CoWoS, CoPoS, 
SoIC, WMCM) and lower capex efficiency in its US operation, our bottom up model shows 
TSMC’s capex could be US$83bn/US$90bn in 2027/28 at a CAGR of 20% CAGR from 2026-28.

Capacity expansion accelerates across 
advanced front-end and back-end 
As TSMC is in the process to retire a portion of its equipment on 6”, 8” and mature 12” 
processes while still adding the capacity on the advanced nodes, we estimate the 
company’s total front-end manufacturing capacity will drop by 2% YoY in 2026. 
Exhibit 17: TSMC's year-end capacity by node for 20nm and below 
TSMC will expand aggressively on 3nm and 2nm through 2027

Source: Company Data, BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Exhibit 18: TSMC's advanced node capacity as % of its installed base 
TSMC's advanced nodes will reach 40% of its total capacity

Source: Company Data, BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

The trend could be more stable into 2027-28 as TSMC strives to optimize its capacity 
mix and across node conversion to accommodate its customers’ demand especially on 
the leading-edge technologies, with its advanced capacity set to grow by 10% CAGR 
from 2025-28 balanced by the mature capacity reduction at -8% CAGR. 
Based on our estimate, TSMC’s capacity for 7nm and below nodes will grow from 320k 
WPM per month in 2023 to ~700k WPM by 2028 at a 16% CAGR, through expansion and 
efficient conversion from higher equipment commonality, cross technology planning and 
AI productivity gains (e.g. tool idle time and chamber cleaning schedule optimization). 
This will also support the mix of 7nm and below capacity climb toward 40-45% of its 
total capacity (vs. 24% in 2023). By region, we expect the capacity expansion through 
2027 to enable its overseas fabs to account for 17% of TSMC’s total capacity by 2028 
(vs. 7% in 2020) and similarly for the advanced node capacity (vs. ~0% in 2023), mainly 
in Arizona and Kumamoto. 
Specifically, we believe the company is on track to its target on 25% CAGR capacity 
expansion for its 5nm and 3nm from ’22-27. We model its 3nm capacity will reach 
190k/230k WPM by 4Q26/27. On N2 family, TSMC plans to grow capacity by 70% CAGR 
from ’26-28, with 1st year ramp delivering 45% more capacity vs. 3nm as it rolls out 5 
fabs at once.

### Page 14

Solid technology roadmap and execution 
easing competition concern 
TSMC is pursuing a dual track strategy for its front-end manufacturing, advancing the 
solution for both its consumer and HPC customers. The nodes targeted for consumer 
chipsets requiring cost efficiency and IP reuse will be introduced on an annual basis to 
match its customers’ yearly product launch schedule. For the HPC applications, the 
company targets to roll out a new node with biennial cadence, supporting major leaps in 
performance, transistor density, and power density. 
Exhibit 27: Front-end manufacturing roadmap comparison 
TSMC's advanced node technology roadmap advancing faster vs. Intel and Samsung and has the widest offerings targeted for different applications

Source: BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
This is evidenced in its N2 family and below nodes, with N2U ramping in ’28 for mobile 
applications and A13 node in ’29 which will be backward compatible to A14. It will also 
have A12 node in ’29 integrated SPR to address power integrity and current delivery 
constraint suitable for AI/HPC systems. The strategy along with the continued expansion 
of the derivative offerings for main nodes should position TSMC well to defend its 
market share with its competitors claiming their progress on the advanced 
manufacturing. 
Specifically, Intel’s 18A has been in mass production since 4Q25 for its Panther Lake 
and Clearwater Forest chipsets though yield will not be fully fine tuned until 1Q27 at 25-
30k WPM capacity scale in its Oregon and Arizona fabs. The follow on derivative 
technology, 18A-P, has recently started risk production featuring 9% performance gains 
though may not enter into mass production until 2027 at the earliest. 
Longer term, Intel’s next major upgrade will be on 14A which is guided to start risk 
production in 2028 for commercialization in 2029. It is important to note A14 will be the 
first major node Intel provides with its external foundry customers and 0.9 PDK is 
targeted to be shipped in 4Q26. 
For Samsung, at its recent SAFE Forum in 2Q26, the company reaffirmed four 
generations of 2nm roadmap, including SF2 which has been in production since 2025, 
SF2P in 2026, SF2P+ in 2027-28 and SF2X (no firm date). Notably, the previously 
announced derivative technologies, including SF2Z featuring backside power for HPC 
applications and SF2A for automotive applications, were not included in its latest 
roadmap which should be folded as part of its SF2P+ offerings. With a modest delay for 
its 2nm progress, the company also pushed out its SF1.4 mass production to 2029 (vs. 
original 2027). 
On the capacity and yield planning for its advanced nodes, Samsung noted its SF2 yield 
is still at 50-60% levels but confirmed its SF2P+ tooling has been in installation through 
2026 for mass production in 2027 in its Taylor fab. Its capacity expansion in the US 
along with the existing 20k WPM capacity on SF2 in Korea put the company’s capacity 
installed base on the node at 35k WPM by the end of 2027.

### Page 15

Exhibit 28: TSMC's front-end manufacturing transistor density vs. Intel 
TSMC is widening its transistor density advantage against Intel as technology advances

Source: BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
Based on the capacity planning and technology roadmap, we believe TSMC retains the 
leadership through its roadmap and yield execution and scale. Specifically, the 
company’s transistor density has been outperforming by ~30% for its N3/N3E vs. Intel 
3/18A. With similar production timing and targeted applications, we estimate the 
transistor density gap could be even widened to ~50%/~60% for TSMC’s upcoming 
A16/A12 nodes when comparing with Intel 18A-P/14A. 
In addition to delivering superior specs on the power, performance and area, TSMC’s 
execution reliability has become self-reinforcing: superior yield and on-time delivery on 
the leading edge technologies win customer trust. The profitable business opportunity 
then translates the cash flow funds to the next leg of capex cycle required for further 
technology and capacity lead. In contrast to TSMC’s 150-200k+ WPM capacity scale on 
every major node at mature yield, Samsung and Intel running their business at 15-20% 
of the company’s scale at 60-70% yield is not sufficient to meet their own demand nor 
the stringent requirement from the external customers especially as the opportunity cost 
gets higher on the advanced manufacturing. 
On the back-end packaging and testing solutions, TSMC provides its customers with 
higher complexity of the system level integration across advanced logic dies, CPO 
components and HBM through its CoWoS and SoIC technologies for the compute 
density and efficiency optimization. To accommodate larger size package, it is rolling out 
5.5-reticle CoWoS in ’26 with yield reaching 98%+ and will push the technology to 
9.5x/14x reticle limit in ’27/’28 
Longer term, the company is developing SoW for up to 40-reticle packaging and CoPoS 
potentially for late ‘28 / early ’29 mass deployment. The company is also upgrading CPO 
with COUPE solutions from PCB and substrate to interposer for 10x power efficiency 
uplift and 20x latency reduction though the contribution would still be limited based on 
our estimate at least through ’27 before accelerating to 1% of its sales in ’28 with more 
scale up switch adoption. 
While CoPoS mass ramp is toward ’29, the CoWoS upgrade at good yield should keep its 
position vs. Intel’s EMIB-T currently at 80-85% pilot yield seeing risk of losing business 
if it fails to deliver 95%+ mass production yield by mid-’27.

### Page 16

SpaceX could be an anchor customer for
TSMC, but not co-owning capacity/tech
On top of the competition risk from the incumbent players, the headline of Elon Musk’s
aggressiveness to build Terafab targeting long term 1 terawatt equivalent chipset
capacity for AI compute deployment in the space term also triggers the investor
concerns on TSMC’s positioning. We believe the risk is overstated as there could be a
delay in the qualification process of Intel’s 18A for external customers and 14A due to IP

and manufacturing flow issues based on the recent supply chain feedback. In addition,
the equipment vendors also noted Terafab is only evaluating on equipment spec and
potential procurement rather than placing firm orders. With extending equipment
delivery lead time, this could put the company in an unfavorable position to receive tools
for the initial production ramp in ’29 it scheduled.
In the mid-term, Terafab’s applications under Texas’s JETI program earlier this year

outlined ~US$119bn of planned investment across 4 phases from ’26-36, with ~60% of
it on tool procurement. The spending in ’26-28 would be highly skewed toward facility
construction before it starts to pull in US$6-8bn equipment per annum from ‘29-36.
We estimate the company will need to spend US$600mn+ for every 1k WPM expansion
on the advanced nodes it targets in the US. Factoring the investment it targets to spend
in the next decade, Terafab could only grow its capacity installed base by ~10k WPM per

annum at maximum to 96k WPM front-end manufacturing capacity installed by ’36, well
below TSMC’s scale, even if it can successfully deliver the technology roadmap through
its own R&D effort and Intel partnership. This would prevent Terafab’s operation from
the economies of scale as the node migration continues, keeping TSMC as the major
foundry partner for Tesla/SpaceX.
Exhibit 29: Wafer supply/demand analysis on Elon Musk's AI deployment target
With Terafab's near term delay and limited long term scale, TSMC will continue to be critical for Musk's ambitious AI compute deployment plan

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH
We also analyze the wafer consumption from SpaceX’s annual orbital AI deployment
target in ‘28/’29 of 10GW/100GW and Musk’s long term 1TW target. Assuming the

compute it plans to deploy in ’28-29 is all powered by the most advanced Nvidia’s
Feynman GPUs delivering 6kW per package, this would drive 1.7mn/16.7mn chipset
demand in ’28/’29 respectively which requires 10k/101k monthly advanced wafer
production. From the supply perspective, as Feynman GPU will be built on TSMC’s A16
node, the chipset demand should be fully accommodated. With limited support from
Terafab, SpaceX in this scenario could become one of the largest customers for TSMC

consuming ~20% of its advanced node capacity in ‘29 (vs. ~1% in ’26 and ~2% in ’28).
Longer term, Musk’s ambitious target to deploy 1 terawatt AI compute power annually
would consume 1mn+ wafers per month on the advanced nodes. To have higher level of
the control on capacity sufficiency, Terafab could consider to partner with TSMC though
the likelihood of TSMC licensing technology or operating a fab with Terafab holding a
meaningful share should be low. Specifically, we would note TSMC’s JV fab record is

limited only with WaferTech, SSMC, VIS, JASM, ESMC and AVSM mostly on mature
nodes and it did not co-own a fab with Apple who contributed ~21% of its revenue from
’15-25 mostly on the advanced processes as it keeps full business control and preserves
customer neutrality.

### Page 17

TSMC valuation reasonable and has room 
to re-rate with its HPC customers 
Exhibit 30: TSMC’s P/E valuation band 
TSMC’s share at 16x '27E P/E is undemanding

Source: BofA Global Research estimates, Bloomberg 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Exhibit 31: TSMC’s valuation based on BofA estimates 
TSMC’s valuation of 16x '27E P/E is at the middle of its historical 10-21x 
range

Source: BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

TSMC’s current valuation remains well supported when viewed through both its 
historical context and its relative positioning vs. its customers. In the past decade, 
TSMC’s forward P/E has averaged ~16x with a range of 10-21x through the cycles. 
Exhibit 32: TSMC's P/E trend vs. its customers 
TSMC customers' P/E is now at 45% premium to TSMC vs. 22% average

Source: BofA Global Research, Bloomberg 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Exhibit 33: TSMC's P/E trend vs. its HPC customers 
HPC fabless are the main driver for valuation premium at 100%

Source: BofA Global Research, Bloomberg 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

The relatively more modest valuation improvement for TSMC also contrasts with its 
customers’ more pronounced re-ratings. Based on our analysis of forward P/E spreads, 
TSMC’s major customers usually have a 22% average premium vs. TSMC in the long 
term though the current level is at 45%, suggesting TSMC’s valuation has been lagging.

### Page 18

Specifically, the valuation deviation for TSMC’s HPC customers vs. the company has 
widened with 100-150% valuation premium vs. TSMC at this stage (vs. historical 
average of 53%). We believe this is driven by a surge in valuation for the direct 
beneficiaries from AI data center deployment, including the companies designing GPUs, 
ASICs and networking chipsets. 
Exhibit 34: TSMC's consensus earnings vs. P/E trend 
TSMC's share price strength only driven by earnings upgrade so far

Source: BofA Global Research, Bloomberg 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
It is also noteworthy that, while TSMC’s share performance since 2H23 has been solid, it 
is predominantly driven by the earnings upward revisions rather than multiple expansion. 
The company’s NTM P/E has remained largely stable, only lifted to 18x in the past 2 
years vs. its long-term ~17x levels. The company’s ADR though has been performing 
better as it attracts a broader investor base vs. common share, with its premium still at 
10% (vs. the long term average 8%) and at 20x 2027 consensus P/E. 
With its firmer technology leadership, this should bode well for the company’s earnings 
upgrade cycle and its share should also be re-rated at least 20x NTM P/E (24x P/E for 
ADR factoring the premium), back closer to its customers and the broader ecosystem.

### Page 22

Price objective basis & risk 
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMWF / TSM) 
Our PO for TSMC is NT$3,260 per share (US$620 per ADR), based on 20x 2027E P/E. 
TSMC's valuation has traded in the range of 10-26x PE from 2017. 20x is in the mid to 
high end of the historical range, above the average P/E of 16x. We think a PE above the 
average is justified given TSMC's enhanced industry position and mid-to-high 50% 5-
year CAGR in AI should underpin a rerating

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) greater-than-expected slowdown in global 
smartphone/consumer electronics demand, (2) Intel's potential insourcing strategy and 
ambitions in foundry service, (3) execution risks on advanced nodes, and (4) higher than 
expected tariffs.

Update
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
September  Sales +1% MoM/
+15% YoY
In this report, we focus on Delta's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$65,400mn (+1% MoM/+15% YoY).
• 3Q26 sales came in at NT$197,059mn (+8% QoQ/+31% YoY), largely in line
with  our estimate at NT$201,379mn (+10% QoQ/+34% YoY), but slightly
below the consensus at NT$208,250mn (+14% QoQ/+39% YoY).

Our view:
• We believe the modest shortfall in 3Q26 revenue  vs. the market expectation
of ~10% QoQ, is due to some component supply shortages and the
customer's pull-in schedule.
• Looking ahead, we expect 4Q26 revenue to grow by double-digit % QoQ on
alleviated component supply and solid demand.
• Regarding the USITC Investigation No. 337, we believe near-term impact
should be manageable, but we will continue to monitor the developments on
this matter (see our report here).

• Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

Lucas.Wang@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2875
Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
What’s Changed
Nanya Technology Corp.
(2408.TW) From To
Price Target NT$580.00 NT$600.00
DRAM supply constraints continue. Contract renewals drove
Nanya's 3Q beat.  CXMT's G5 milestone strengthens its tech
roadmap, but yield ramp and customer qualification take time.

Nanya 3Q beat; LTAs in spotlight:  Nanya reported 3Q revenue of NT$134bn, +62%
Q/Q, +612% Y/Y, 8% above consensus. 3Q gross profit was NT$114bn, +114% Q/Q,
12% above consensus, implying a GM of 85.7%. 3Q net profit was NT$88bn, +75%
Q/Q, 9% above consensus, implying a net margin of 65.1%. The 3Q Q/Q revenue
growth was driven mainly by ASP increase, which primarily reflects renewal of the
one-year and six-month L TAs, which were previously negotiated when ASPs were

lower; most  expired in 3Q26. Currently L TAs cover 50-70% of output; only 20% of
output is covered by three-year contracts; Nanya intends to extend the L TA
durations for longer-term supply according to customer demand, and there are
discussions on price floors and ceilings for the new contracts.
Nanya management sees continued DRAM shortage in 4Q and expects that ASP
may continue to improve: The supply constraint is the long lead time for new
capacity, and the undersupply is unlikely to be resolved in the near term. Nanya

targets capex  of NT$69.7bn in 2026 and NT$346bn  for the 35.9kwpm capacity of its
new Fab 5A from 2026-29. Management estimates that AI infra currently accounts
for ~20% of its revenue mix, but that could increase to 40-50% by 2029/30.
CXMT updates: On September 7 during Q&A, CXMT reiterated expectations for
tight DRAM supply in 2H26; its response on Apple expressed openness to global
customers without confirming a supply agreement. On September 21, after its G5

mass production announcement, CXMT clarified that G5 sales are not yet at scale,
while yield ramp-up and customer qualification would take time. Based on our
checks, CXMT's HBM3E yield is already 40%, and DRAM wafer input should ramp to
30kwpm by end-2027.
Constrained DRAM supply is the industry consensus: The 4QF26 earnings call for
Micron  (covered by Joseph Moore) highlighted that  persistent supply constraints are
expected through CY2027-28. It cited industry-wide clean room constraints owing to

lengthy construction; most of the increase in Micron's F27 capex (vs. its previous
plan) is allocated to construction, targeting clean room availability in late CY28 and
beyond. Micron also expects rising industry HBM wafer requirements to limit bit
growth, forecasting industry DRAM bit shipment growth of low-20s (%) in CY27-28.
Samsung (covered by Shawn Kim) also expects HBM to absorb nearly 30% of
industry DRAM wafer capacity in 2027, vs ~20% currently.

Stock implications:
• Nanya Tech (OW):  Price target raised to NT$600; 2026e, 2027e, and 2028e
EPS raised  22%, 18%, and 19%, respectively.
•  CXMT (OW): Base case value/price target unchanged at Rmb88.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Morgan Stanley Research 5
Nanya Tech: Valuation Methodology
We raise our price target (base case scenario value) from NT$580 to NT$600: Our
price target reflects a BVPS multiple of 4.11x for 2026 (was 4.77x) versus ROE of 76% for
2026.  We lower our target multiple to reflect recent memory segment volatility. Our
2026e BVPS estimates is raised to NT$146.00 from NT$121.52. We expect DDR4 pricing
strength and L TAs to drive a re-rating.
We raise our bull and bear case values from NT$1,230 and NT$320 to NT$1,270 and NT

$330, respectively, implying 8.70x and 2.26x 2026e BVPS.

CXMT: Valuation Methodology
We keep our base case value/price target unchanged: We continue to apply RI
methodology to derive our price target. The price target did not change despite our
2026/27 earnings estimate increases because the contribution to calculated value from
our RI model comes mostly from the subsequent forecast period  and the continuing value.
Key RI assumptions remain unchanged, including cost of equity of 9.9%, , intermediate

growth rate of 11.0%, terminal growth rate of 3.0%, and cash dividend payout ratio of 51%,
.
Our base case value/price target now implies 14.3x 2027e EPS vs. our prior target multiple
of 18.4x 2027e BVPS, as we see a stronger DRAM market outlook given the continued
supply constraint; we also believe the G5 breakthrough reduces risks around CXMT's
technology roadmap.
Our bull case value remains unchanged at Rmb132; it now implies 21.5x our 2027e EPS.

We adjust our bear case value to Rmb40 from Rmb70 to account for recent volatility; it
now implies 6.5x 2027e EPS.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates

from 180k wpm in 2025 to 500k by 2028,

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

기아 (000270) 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결

▶Analyst 김성래 sr.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7751

Buy (유지) 
목표주가(하향): 260,000원

현재 주가(10/6) 112,600원 
상승여력 ▲130.9%

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

2026 年 10 月 14 日 全13 頁

AI の雇用への影響をどう読むか 
一部の職種で狭まる若手の「入口」、経験を積む場をどう残すか

AI アナリティックリサーチ室 主任研究員 新田 尭之

[要約] 
 近年の実証研究では、米国を中心に、生成AI の影響を受けやすい一部の職種で若手の
雇用悪化が相次いで報告されている。悪化は主に採用の減少として表れており、最初
の職を得る「入口」が狭まっている。ただし、悪化の一部は ChatGPT 公開前に始まっ
ており、在宅勤務の広がりやコロナ禍の採用増の反動とも重なる。複数の要因が考え
られる中、AI の影響の大きさについて明確な結論は得られていない。 
 若手の「入口」が狭まる一方、米国全体では雇用者数が増えている。もっとも、 雇用
増の多くは民間教育・医療によるもので、幅広い産業には広がっていない。採用も離
職も少ないため、既に職に就いている人は職を失いにくいが、これから職を得る人や
転職を望む人にとっては空席が少ない。さらに、AI による省力化は、人員を減らすよ
り、業務の中身や外注を見直す形で進む例が多い。雇用者数だけでは、こうした仕事
の機会の変化は見えにくい。 
 今後の雇用を分けるのは、AI による値下げや品質向上で需要が広がったり、新しい業
務が生まれたりして、どれだけ人手が必要になるかである。ただし、新たな仕事が生
まれても、若手に回るとは限らない。41 カ国の多国籍企業の分析では、生成AI を導入
した企業の拠点で経験者の雇用が増え、若手の比率が下がった。経験者の多くは、入
口の仕事を通じて育つ。若手の採用を絞る判断は、個々の企業には合理的でも、続け
ば将来の経験者が育たない「育成の空洞化」を招きかねない。若手が経験を積む場を
どう残すかが問われており、社内で人を育てる日本企業も無縁ではいられないだろう。

### Page 2

2 / 13

1. はじめに 
近年、生成 AI の進化や普及を背景に、AI が雇用に与える影響について、米国を中心に実証
研究が蓄積されている（図表1）。 
図表 1 生成 AI が雇用に与える影響に関する近年の主な実証研究

職種。 
（出所）各研究より大和総研作成

### Page 3

1

3 / 13

例えば、スタンフォード大学の研究者らは、給与計算・人事サービスを手掛ける ADP の給与
記録を用いて米国の労働市場を分析し、主に次の 6 点を報告している 1。①AI による経済全体
にわたる広範な雇用喪失は確認されない。②一方、AI の影響を受けやすい職種では、受けにく
い職種に比べて22～25 歳の雇用の伸びが弱く、年長層には同程度の差はみられない。③この若
年層の職種間格差は2026 年半ばにかけて拡大を続けた。④その差は主に離職の増加ではなく、
採用の減少による。⑤雇用の減少はAI が人の作業を代替する使い方の多い職種に集中し、仕事
を補完する使い方では雇用の横ばい・増加との関連がみられる。 ⑥調整は基本給よりも雇用の
面に表れている。 
こうした結果は、AI が雇用に与える影響を、全体の人数だけでなく、年齢や採用・離職、AI
の使われ方などに分けて捉える必要性を示している。そこで、本稿ではこうした実証研究や統
計データを基に、①雇用の悪化が目立つ層と、そのうちAI の影響と読める範囲（第 2 章） 、②
雇用者数だけでは捉えにくい仕事の機会の変化（第3 章 ）、 ③今後の雇用を分ける要因（第4 章）
を検討する。

2. 悪化は「入口」で目立つが、AI だけでは説明できない 
（1）AI の影響を受けやすい職種の若年層を中心に悪化 
近年の実証研究を見ると、米国の若年層で雇用の悪化が目立つ。前述のスタンフォード大学
の研究では、22～25 歳の雇用者数は2026 年6 月にChatGPT 公開時の2022 年11 月を約2％下回
った一方、26 歳以上の各年齢層では増加し、特に35～40 歳では約11％多い（図表2）。 
図表 2 年齢層別に見た雇用者数の推移（米国、2022 年 11 月＝1）

（注）ADP の給与記録のうち、2021 年 1 月～2026 年 6 月に継続して観測される企業の 70 歳未満のフルタイム労
働者（毎月 350 万～500 万人） 。年齢層別人口の変化は未調整。 
（出所）Brynjolfsson, Chandar, and Chen [2026]より大和総研作成（画像の加工・仮訳は大和総研）

1 Brynjolfsson, Chandar, and Chen [2026]. AI 曝露はEloundou et al. [2024] の指標のうち GPT-4 による
判定の値で、職業を五つの群に分けて比べている。

### Page 4

1

4 / 13

ただし、若年層の雇用が職種を問わず悪化したわけではない。同研究の職種別の推計では、
AI への曝露（仕事の内容がAI で代替・支援されうる度合い）が最も高い職種群の22～25 歳の
雇用の伸びは、2022 年11 月から2026 年6 月までに、最も低い職種群の雇用の伸びを約18％ポ
イント下回った。若年層の中でも、AI の影響を受けやすい職種で悪化が目立つ。 
さらに、米国国勢調査局の研究者らは、大学の卒業記録と雇用の行政記録を 接続し、専攻別
に卒業生の就職状況を比べた。これによると、 AI への曝露が最も高い専攻の卒業生は、
ChatGPT 公開直後から、卒業翌四半期に就業している割合が曝露の低い専攻の卒業生と比べて
低下し始め、2024 年以降は約5％ポイント低い2。 
米国の約6,500 万人分の職歴データを企業単位で分析した研究でも、生成AI の導入企業では
若手（職位の低い層）の雇用が2023 年初めから6 四半期で比較対象の企業より約9％減った。
これも主に採用の減少によるという 3。求人の側では、求人サイト Indeed の分析で、テック職
で 5 年以上の経験を求める割合の上昇が確認されている4。 
ニューヨーク連銀が集計したデータからも、若年大卒者の悪化は確認できる。22～27 歳の若
年大卒者の失業率は、2018 年までほぼ一貫して全労働者を下回っていたが、2019 年以降はおお
むね上回るようになり、2026 年 6 月は 5.7％と全労働者（4.1％）を 1.6％ポイント上回る。大
卒でない若年層（22～27 歳）の失業率が 2025 年秋を頂点に下がり始めた後も、若年大卒者の
失業率は高止まりしている（図表3）5。 
図表 3 全労働者と若年層の失業率（米国）

（注）季節調整値の3 か月移動平均。全労働者は16～65 歳、大卒でない若年層は22～27 歳の学士未満、若年大
卒者は 22～27 歳の学士以上で、いずれも在学中の人を除く。 
（出所）ニューヨーク連銀 “The Labor Market for Recent College Graduates”（2026 年8 月6 日）     
より大和総研作成

2 Orr, Tucker, and Warren [2026]. AI 曝露が上位1 割の専攻と、AI 曝露が下位6 割の専攻の比較で、就業は
失業保険の対象となる雇用の行政記録で把握. 
3 Hosseini Maasoum and Lichtinger [2026]. 導入企業は、生成AI の業務導入を担う人材（GenAI 
integrator）の求人から判定。同研究のジュニア・シニアは、Revelio Labs が職名・職歴・年齢などから推定
した職位の区分であり、年齢層の区分ではない。 
4 Bernard [2025]. 経験要件に言及したテック職の求人のうち、5 年以上の経験を求める割合は2022 年 4～6
月の 37％から 2025 年同期に 42％へ上昇した（テック以外は 16％から 11％に低下）。 
5 Federal Reserve Bank of New York “The Labor Market for Recent College Graduates”August 6, 2026

### Page 5

1

5 / 13

若年大卒者の失業率を専攻別に見ると、2024 年の失業率はコンピューター工学が 7.8％、コ
ンピューター科学が 7.0％と、全専攻（4.2％）を大きく上回り、73 専攻の上位 5 位以内に入
る。一方、就職後の年収中央値は 9 万ドル、8.7 万ドルと全専攻（5.8 万ドル）を大きく上回
り、大卒資格を要しない職に就く不完全就業率も低い。就業者の年収水準の高さと、失業率の
高さが併存している（図表4）。 
図表 4 専攻別に見た若年大卒者の失業率と年収中央値（米国、2024 年）

タイム就業者。赤はコンピューター系の専攻。

なお、オンラインのフリーランス市場でも、AI の影響を受ける分野では、補完的とみなされ
る技能の分野を含め、経験の浅い人向けの案件が、影響を受けない分野に比べて相対的に減っ
たと報告されている 6。年齢と経験の浅さは同じ区分ではないが、若手の 雇用悪化と経験の浅
い人向けの案件の減少は、最初の仕事を得る機会、いわば「入口」が狭まっている点で共通す
る。 
（2）ChatGPT 公開前からの悪化や在宅勤務の広がりなども重なる 
ただし、こうした悪化の原因すべてを生成AI の影響に帰するのは早計であろう。第一に、悪
化の一部は ChatGPT 公開前に始まっていた。例えば、米国のソフトウェア開発職の求人は、
2022 年 2 月をピークに急減し、ChatGPT 公開時には既にピークを3 分の 1 あまり下回っていた
（図表5）。 
ニューヨーク連銀のブログに掲載された求人データの分析でも、AI 曝露の高い職種の求人の
相対的な減少は2022 年より前に始まっており、2022 年以降に傾向の明確な変化は見られない。

6 Teutloff et al. [2025]. 受注・収入の減少はHui, Reshef, and Zhou [2024] も参照。

同じ分析では、高曝露職の中で若手（職位の低い層）向けと経験者向けの求人がほぼ並行して
動いており、求人の減少は入口の仕事に集中していないという 7。 
図表 5 ソフトウェア開発職の求人指数（米国）

第二に、在宅勤務の広がりである。ニューヨーク連銀のブログに掲載された分析によると、
米国の在宅勤務が可能な職業では、若手と年長の大卒者の失業率の差が2019 年から 2024 年に
約 1.4％ポイント広がった 8。これは、在宅勤務が難しい職業の同期間の拡大幅（約0.5％ポイ
ント）を大きく上回る。同分析は、在宅勤務では若手への OJT や上司からの助言が難しくなる
ため、企業が経験の浅い人材の採用をためらいやすくなると指摘している。 米英加豪の採用記
録約 2.4 億件の分析でも、在宅勤務のしやすさを同時に考慮すると、AI 曝露と採用者に占める
若手（職位の低い層）の比率との関係は大きく弱まり、多くの場合、統計的に有意 な関係は確
認できなくなった 9。 
米国外の研究も、若手の雇用の減少を AI だけでは説明していない。デンマークでは、AI 曝
露の高いいくつかの職で若手の雇用が減ったものの、研究者らはその減少はAI チャットボット
を導入した企業によるものではないとしている 10。さらに、韓国銀行の研究者の分析によれば、
韓国では 2022 年 6 月から 2026 年 6 月に 15～29 歳の雇用が 28.5 万人減り、その 94％は AI の
影響を受けやすい産業で生じた 11。同研究は、コロナ禍の採用増の反動や経験者中心の採用、
在宅勤務の広がりなども作用した可能性があると指摘している。

7 Audoly, Guerin, and Topa [2026]. 求人情報などを収集・分析するLightcast の米国の求人データを用い、
AI 曝露はAnthropic が作成した、AI の実際の利用状況を反映した作業単位の指標による。若手（職位の低い
層）向け・経験者向けの区分は Lightcast が求人の記載から判定したもの。求人の数の分析であり、実際に誰
が採用されたかは捉えない点で、第 2 章（1）の研究とは対象が異なる。 
8 Emanuel, Harrington, and Pallais [2026]. 大卒者の18～28 歳と29 歳以上の失業率の差の2019 年からの
変化で、数値は同記事のチャートデータによる。 
9 Lambert and Schindler [2026]. 米英加豪の2017～2025 年のデータで、分析対象は、職名と職歴から判定し
た職位の下位 2 区分が採用者に占める比率。 
10 Humlum and Vestergaard [2026]. AI の影響を受けやすい 11 職業の約 2.5 万人への調査を2024 年 12 月まで

そして先に紹介したスタンフォード大学の研究では 、在宅勤務や金利への感応度を調整して
も、22～25 歳の高曝露職と低曝露職の雇用の伸びの差は大きく変わらないが、学歴構成も含め
て調整するとその差は調整前の約 18％ポイントから約 8％ポイントに縮み、統計的に有意な関
係は確認できなくなる12。 
以上のように、一部の職種で若手の雇用が悪化していることは確認できるが、その原因を生
成 AI に求めるには慎重さが必要である。その影響を見極めるには、ChatGPT 公開前からの悪化
と、公開後に加わった可能性のある変化を分けて考える必要がある。また 、足元の弱さをコロ
ナ禍の採用増の反動だけで説明できるとは限らない。複数の要因が考えられる中で、本稿で取
り上げた研究でも、AI の影響の大きさについて明確な結論は得られていない。

3. 雇用者数だけでは捉えにくい仕事の機会の変化 
仕事を得る機会を考えるには、雇用者数が増えているかどうかに加え、どの分野で増えてい
るのか、新たな採用がどの程度行われているのかを見る必要がある。また、AI による省力化も、
人員数を変えずに業務や外注の見直しとして進む場合がある。以下では、これら三つの側面を
確認する。 
（1）雇用増は民間教育・医療に偏る 
米国では、ChatGPT が公開された2022 年 11 月から2026 年 8 月までに、情報や専門・事業サ
ービスなど、AI の影響を受けやすい職種の多い産業で雇用は減少した。一方、全体の雇用増を
支えたのは民間教育・医療である。同期間の雇用増約 480 万人のうち、約 3 分の 2（約 325 万
人）を占めた。ただし、直近 1 年の全体の雇用増は約 60 万人にとどまり、そのうち約 54 万人
を民間教育・医療が占める（図表6）。 
図表 6 業種別に見た雇用者数の増減（米国）

（注）その他は全体から掲載した業種を差し引いた残差。 
（出所）米労働統計局（BLS）より大和総研作成

12 この研究では、学歴構成は AI が仕事に影響する経路も反映している可能性があるため、その違いを調整し
て差が縮んでも、AI 以外の要因で説明できたとは限らない、としている。

### Page 8

1

8 / 13

民間教育・医療の雇用者数の伸びは、コロナ禍で落ち込んだ反動にもみえる。ただ、コロナ
禍前の2020 年2 月から見ても、雇用増約680 万人の半分（約340 万人）を占めており、雇用増
の最大の担い手であることは変わらない。全体の雇用増が、幅広い産業での雇用拡大を意味す
るわけではない。 
（2）低採用・低離職の下で就職・転職の機会は限られる 
雇用者数の動きと、新たに職を得る機会の違いは、採用と離職からも確認できる。 米国の求
人労働異動調査（JOLTS）によると、2026 年 1～7 月の採用率は平均 3.3％と、コロナ禍前の
2017～19 年（3.8％）を下回り、失業率が 7～8％台だった 2012～13 年並みに低い（図表 7）。
同じ期間の比較では、離職率も3.7％から3.3％に下がり、自己都合退職率（2.2％→2.0％）も
解雇率（1.2％→1.1％）も低下した。採用率の低さは若手だけの現象ではなく、労働市場全体
にみられる。 
図表 7 採用率と離職率（米国）

（出所）BLS より大和総研作成

離職が少なければ、欠員を埋める採用も生まれにくい。就職・転職の機会が限られる中、 先
行きへの不安から今の職にとどまる動きは「ジョブハギング（仕事にしがみつくこと） 」と呼
ばれ、2025 年 8 月ごろから米国の報道で広く取り上げられている13。米国の履歴書作成サイト
が2026 年2 月に行った調査では、自らをジョブハギングの状態にあると回答した就業者が57％
と、半年前の 45％から増えた。その 7 割は、今後半年以内に AI が自分の雇用に影響すること
を懸念していた 14。就職の入口に立つ人にとっては、いわば「空席の少ない市場」である。 
（3）AI による省力化の影響は、人員の削減より仕事の中身の見直しに表れている 
AI による省力化についても、雇用者数だけでは変化を捉えにくい。OECD が日本を含む7 カ国

13 例えば、Fortune “Workers are ‘job hugging’ in a stagnant labor market, but growing resentment 
means they could bail as soon as the next Great Resignation comes”（2025 年8 月 18 日）。 
14 ResumeBuilder.com “6 in 10 Workers Are Clinging To Their Jobs as Job Hugging Soars in 2026”
（2026 年2 月11 日）。米国の就業者2,188 人へのオンライン調査で、ジョブハギングを、会社への愛着では
なく不安から今の職にとどまることとしている。

の中小企業に行った調査では、生成 AI を使う企業の 83％が人員の必要数に影響はないと答え
た。必要な人員数の減少（9％）より、従業員の業務負担（33％）や外注への依存（14％）の
減少を挙げる企業が多い 15。社内の人員数が変わらなくても、外部に依頼する仕事は減りうる。 
前出のデンマークの研究では、時間を節約できた利用者の 85％が、その時間を他の業務に充
てると答え、雇用主が特段の施策を講じていない職場でも、利用者の8％が AI の出力の品質確
認など全く新しい業務を担っていた。研究者らは、こうした状況が、業務の見直しなど計測さ
れにくい投資が先行し、生産性の伸びが遅れて表れる「J カーブ」の初期段階と整合的だとし
ている 16。AI による省力化が、直ちに人員や労働時間の削減につながるのではなく、仕事の配
分を変える形で進んでいることがうかがえる。

4. 新たな仕事が生まれても、若手に回るとは限らない 
第 2 章で紹介した研究では、若手の雇用悪化は、主に「解雇」ではなく「採用されない」形
で表れている。この状態が続くかは、AI による省力化で生まれる余力を企業がどの業務に振り
向け、新たな需要がどれだけ人手を必要とするかにかかっている。 
省力化の後の展開は、主に三つの経路として整理できる 17。①人数を変えずに仕事の中身が
変わる。②既存の社員で対応できる範囲が広がり、採用・外注の抑制や人員削減につながる。
③値下げや品質向上による需要の拡大、新しい業務の創出が人手を必要とし、配置転換や採用
につながる。 
AI 導入後の雇用の動きには、企業規模による違いも見られる。デンマークの研究では、AI を
導入した小規模企業で新たな採用が抑えられ、雇用の増えるペースが鈍ったと報告されている。
一方、大規模企業では、同様の雇用の伸びの鈍化は確認されなかった18。 
②と③は、同じ企業の中で同時に進みうる。41 カ国の多国籍企業を対象とした研究は、AI導
入企業で増えた雇用がどの層に向かったかを示している。生成AI を導入した企業の拠点では、
ChatGPT 公開直後から若手（職位の低い層）の比率が下がり始め、2026 年 3 月には導入してい
ない企業の拠点での比率を 1.9％ポイント下回った（図表 8）19。この比率低下の主因は、若手

下の企業 5,232 社（2024 年10～12 月）。割合は生成AI を利用する企業の回答で、一時点の自己申告による。

とどまり、日本単独の推計は示されていない。Hosseini Maasoum and Lichtinger [2026] は、同じ Revelio
の職歴データを用いて米国の導入企業で若手の雇用減を示しており、対象や推計方法により結果は異なる。

の雇用減少ではなく、ChatGPT 公開から約 1 年半後に始まった経験者の雇用増加である。この
研究の著者らは、AI の影響を受けやすい職種では、AI が若手の仕事を省く一方で経験者への需
要を広げたと解釈している。需要が広がっても、その担い手が若手になるとは限らないことを
示す結果である。 
図表 8 生成 AI を導入した企業の海外拠点での雇用の変化（41 カ国）

公開（2022 年 11 月） 。若手は職歴データ（Revelio Labs）の職位区分 1～2、経験者は 3～7（年齢層の区分で

③の経路を示す研究もある。米国の上場企業では、AI 投資を増やした企業ほど、主に製品の
革新を通じて売上と雇用が伸びたとされる 20。デンマークの研究で報告された、AI の出力の品
質確認などの新しい業務も③の芽といえる。 
採用の構成も、AI の使い方によって変わりうる。AI を使う既存社員が若手の入口の仕事を担
えば、採用は経験者に寄りうる。逆に、AI の支援で経験の浅い人に任せられる仕事が増えれば、

20 Babina et al. [2024]. 2010～2018 年の非テック上場企業の分析。効果は規模の大きい企業に集中する。他
社からの需要の移転も含みうるが、AI 投資の多い産業では産業全体でも売上と雇用が増えたとされる。

### Page 11

1

11 / 13

若手を採る利点は高まりうる。AI が経験の差を縮めるこうした作用は、「専門性の平準化」と
呼ばれる 21。 
もっとも、これまでの実証研究では、採用が経験者に寄る動きが目立つ。この 動きが続けば、
懸念されるのは「育成の空洞化」である。 経験者の多くは、入口の仕事を通じて育つ。若手採
用の絞り込みは、個々の企業には合理的でも、経済全体では将来の経験者層 を薄くしかねない。
AI の支援と経験者の指導を組み合わせ、若手が経験を積む場を残す工夫が求められる。 
日本企業もこの問題と無縁ではいられないだろう。日本では少子高齢化で人手不足が続き、
新卒を一括採用して社内で育てる企業が多い。このため、影響は採用数より、採る職種の構成
や、人手の足りない部署への配置転換に先に表れる可能性がある 22。とはいえ、採用数が保た
れても、AI が入口の仕事を担えば、若手が経験を積む場は細りうる。問われているのは「AI で
若手の仕事をどこまで省けるか」ではなく、 「AIを使いながら若手をどう育てるか」である。

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -30.55 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dệt may, Tiện ích khí và Bao bì & đóng gói là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị  
-2,865 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã VHM, VPB và VIC; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -146 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -3,351 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua nhưng 
đang duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 1,650 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 2,822 hợp đồng nhưng 
đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,636 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam bước vào tuần đầu tiên ở vị thế thị trường mới nổi bằng một nhịp điều 
chỉnh, khi VN-Index để mất mốc 1.800 điểm và lùi về đóng cửa quanh 1.785 điểm. Đây là 
phản ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" khá điển hình: ngay trong phiên nâng hạng chính 
thức 21/9, chỉ số mở cửa hưng phấn rồi nhanh chóng đảo chiều do áp lực chốt lời, với 
nhóm cổ phiếu Vingroup (VIC, VHM, VRE) giảm mạnh dẫn dắt đà đi xuống, và sức ép lan 
rộng sang bất động sản, ngân hàng, năng lượng về cuối tuần. Điểm đáng chú ý là dòng 
vốn ngoại đã tiếp tục bán ròng ngay sau cột mốc ngày 21/9, dù đã mua ròng trở lại trong 
tuần trước đó. Bối cảnh bên ngoài cũng kém thuận lợi khi Fed vừa tăng lãi suất và phát đi 
tín hiệu diều hâu, chứng khoán Mỹ điều chỉnh, giá dầu neo cao, và lợi suất trái phiếu 
Chính phủ Mỹ tăng vọt. Trong một, hai tuần tới, bên cạnh các yếu tố ngoại biên, mùa báo 
cáo KQKD quý 3 cũng sẽ bắt đầu tác động lên diễn biến thị trường chung.

Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ tăng 
trưởng khả quan trong 2Q26 14% 
YoY, với động lực chính đến từ mảng 
dịch vụ CNTT nước ngoài

Mảng CNTT nước ngoài: Lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ IT phân cực mạnh mẽ do tác nhân 
AI (AI Agentic) s ẽ củng c ố xu hư ớng 
đầu tư vào AI

FPT ở vị thế thuận lợi cho phép 
doanh nghiệp đa dạng hóa công cụ 
AI và tối ưu chi phí vận hành

Khuyến ngh ị MUA với giá m ục tiêu 
86,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Do không còn h ạch toán FPT Telecom (FOX) là công ty con, d oanh thu 2Q2026 của 
FPT giảm mạnh còn 13,810 tỷ VND (-17% YoY). Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ không 
bị ảnh hưởng bởi thay đổi hạch toán đạt 2,568 tỷ VND (14% YoY). Mảng dịch vụ CNTT 
tại thị trường nước ngoài tiếp tục là đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chính, đạt doanh thu và 
LNTT lần lượt là 9,868/1,538 tỷ VND (16.3% /19.3% YoY).

Sự chuyển dịch chi tiêu từ dịch vụ IT truyền thống sang chuyển đổi số và tích hợp AI 
được kỳ vọng sẽ diễn ra ngày m ột mạnh mẽ khi: i) Sự phát triển của năng l ực tính 
toán giúp kéo giảm chi phí token (đơn v ị năng lực tính toán của AI) và thúc đ ẩy nhu 
cầu sử dụng AI của doanh nghiệp; ii) Các mô hình AI mã nguồn mở gia tăng lựa chọn 
cho người dùng sẽ thúc đẩy chi tiêu

Đối với các doanh nghiệp ứng dụng sâu các mô hình AI phục vụ hoạt động kinh doanh 
như FPT , chúng tôi nhận thấy xu hướng tối ưu chi phí tốt hơn nhờ khả năng sử dụng 
các mô hình AI đ ến từ đa dạng nhà cung c ấp; với chi phí trên hi ệu năng tính toán 
được cải thi ện. Trong k ịch b ản cơ s ở, chúng tôi d ự báo biên l ợi nhuận trước thu ế 
mảng CNTT nước ngoài sẽ ổn định ở mức 15.4% nhờ duy trì chi ến lược quản trị chi 
phí hiệu quả qua đó giúp LNTT 2026/2027 đạt 6,198/7,087 tỷ VND (13.3%/14.3% YoY).

Dựa trên định giá FCFF và P/E, triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các rủi ro có 
thể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu FPT . Giá mục tiêu là 86,000 
VND/cổ phiếu.

nhẹ nhờ mức tăng của mảng điện gió 
sau khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 vận 
hành

Mảng M&E ghi nhận giá trị ký mới cao 
trong 1H.2026, t ạo kỳ vọng hồi phục 
mạnh mẽ doanh thu

Mảng cho thuê văn phòng đứng trước 
cơ hội lớn đóng góp vào tăng trư ởng 
nhờ các điều kiện thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
58,100 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Quý 2/2026 giảm nhẹ, đạt 2,432 tỷ VND (-3% YoY/-2% QoQ), trong khi l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ đạt 632 tỷ VND (+1% YoY/-11.5% QoQ), do doanh thu của 
mảng bất động sản suy giảm. Qua đó, REE hoàn thành lần lượt 40%/47.8% kế hoạch 
doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ 2026.

Trong Q2.2026, tổng sản lượng phát đi ện của REE tăng nh ẹ, đạt 2.8 tỷ kWh (+2.3% 
YoY) với đóng góp tăng trưởng rõ nét đến từ mảng điện gió khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 
bắt đầu vận hành; tuy nhiên  sản lượng thủy điện suy gi ảm do tác đ ộng tiêu c ực từ 
pha thời tiết El Nino, đạt 1.1 tỷ kWh.

Với năng lực chuyên môn được nâng cao, mảng M&E ghi nhận những dấu hiệu phục 
hồi tích cực trong giá trị hợp đồng ký mới trong 1H.2026, 5,865 tỷ VND (cao hơn 53.9% 
so với giá trị ký mới cả năm 2025). KBSV dự phóng doanh thu c ủa mảng M&E sẽ đạt 
4,499/5,190 tỷ VND (+28%/15% YoY) trong 2026/2027.

Hội tụ bởi 3 yếu tố: i) Định hướng phát triển kinh tế Hồ Chí Minh trở thành trung tâm 
tài chính quốc tế; ii) Hạ tầng giao thông ngày một được hoàn thiện và iii) Chi phí thuê 
cạnh tranh so với chất lượng dịch vụ của E. Town 6, KBSV dự phóng doanh thu mảng 
cho thuê văn phòng trong 2026/2027 tăng trưởng +14%/6.2% YoY.

Dựa trên đ ịnh giá SOTP , triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các r ủi ro có th ể 
phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu REE. Giá m ục tiêu là 58,100 
VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 28.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa tại ngày 15/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q.26 bứt phá nhờ khoản 
thu nhập bất thường của nhiệt điện 
Cà Mau và Vũng Áng

Nhiệt đi ện than duy trì công su ất ở 
mức cao nh ờ nguồn cung nhiên li ệu 
ổn định trong 2H26

Điện khí Đông Nam Bộ (NT1&2 và 
NT3&4) có triển vọng hoạt động 
thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
18,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Quý 2/2026, POW ghi nhận Doanh thu và LNST cổ đông công ty mẹ đạt lần lượt 
20,308 tỷ VND (116.1% YoY) và 3,519 tỷ VND (484.6% YoY) chủ yếu nhờ các khoản thu 
nhập từ chênh lệch tiền khí của nhiệt điện Cà Mau và tỷ giá của nhiệt điện Vũng Áng. 
Với kết qu ả trên, POW hoàn thành l ần lư ợt 65%/420% kế hoạch doanh thu và l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ.

Chúng tôi cho r ằng nhà máy nhi ệt than Vũng Áng trong 2026 s ẽ được duy trì công 
suất huy động ở mức cao nhờ: i) Xác suất diễn ra El Nino tăng cao và dự kiến kéo dài 
đến đầu 2027, ii) Nguồn cung than trong nước được bảo đảm từ các đối tác (TKV, TCT 
Đông B ắc) và d ự trữ than d ồi dào c ủa doanh nghi ệp ghi nh ận ở mức cao trong 
Q2/2026.

Dự án đường dây 500kV Mạch 3 đi vào vận hành giúp giảm áp lực cạnh tranh của các 
nhà máy điện tại khu vực phía Nam, qua đó kỳ vọng doanh thu của NT1&2 trong năm 
2026 đạt 11,104 tỷ VND (5.3% YoY). Đối với dự án điện NT3&4, khoản thu nhập chênh 
lệch sản lượng theo giá công suất do sản lượng điện thực tế thấp hơn so với tỷ lệ Qc 
65% của nhà máy được duy trì sang 2H26 và 2027 sẽ giúp cho dự án ghi nhận doanh 
thu lần lượt 17,848/17,621 tỷ VND doanh thu trong 2026/2027.

Dựa trên định giá SOTP và EV/EBITDA, tri ển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các 
rủi ro có th ể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu POW. Giá m ục 
tiêu là 18,100 VND/cổ phiếu.

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trư ởng tích c ực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trư ởng khách qua c ảng hàng 
không thúc đ ẩy doanh thu t ại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ c ấu kinh doanh và hi ệu qu ả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nh ận doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 479/92 t ỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng ch ờ thương gia. Lũy k ế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 1,035/221 t ỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác tăng 
bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế gia tăng 
được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi khách, đặc biệt 
tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 182 
điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp t ừ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nh ất và 6 điểm mới tại T2 
Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Qu ốc và Gia Bình ti ếp tục mở ra 
dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, phòng 
chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh đ ược kỳ vọng tác động 
tích c ực lên biên l ợi nhu ận của AST . Chúng tôi dự phóng biên l ợi nhu ận g ộp đ ạt 
64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 48%/17% 
yoy.

Chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với AST , giá mục tiêu 76 ,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh đư ợc 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng hơn; 
trong khi tri ển v ọng nhóm khách 
hàng công nghi ệp b ị ảnh hư ởng b ởi 
bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của 
mảng này

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 
tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An v ận hành từ Q3/2025 giúp 
sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài c ủa các đ ợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ s ở để 
chúng tôi đi ều chỉnh tăng tăng trư ởng tiêu th ụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phư ớc trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế quan 
có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xu ất khẩu truyền thống của Việt 
Nam.

UBND TP .HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng mạnh 
từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh trong thực 
tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự án của doanh 
nghiệp ở mảng này s ẽ vận hành hi ệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và l ợi nhuận gộp 
của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ VND lần lượt trong 
2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với BWE, 
giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng 
cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX điều chỉnh trở lại trong tuần qua sau khi gặp thử thách tại vùng cản quanh 1850, 
hình thành cây nến tuần giảm điểm nhưng với phần bóng dưới khá dài cho thấy sự tham 
gia tích cực của lực cầu bắt đáy tại vùng hỗ trợ gần. Trên khung ngày, nhịp giảm mạnh giữa 
tuần đã khiến chỉ số đánh mất đáy ngắn hạn tuần trước và chớm cắt xuống dưới SMA200, 
trong khi phiên hồi phục cuối tuần chưa cho thấy sự cải thiện rõ rệt của lực cầu. Mặc dù 
diễn biến này cho thấy áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng 
tăng ngắn hạn chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại. 
Kháng cự xa: 1880 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1830 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1710 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

trưởng 6%-8%/năm

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
IMF ANNUAL MEETINGS
Resilience, but no relief. The IMF's latest global growth forecast is un-
changed from a year ago and from April 2026 (at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.4%
in 2027), despite the energy shock, however with a different geogra -
phical breakdown: many energy importers and war-exposed countries
downgraded, and AI-exposed countries upgraded. Resilience was the

buzzword of the meetings, however it appeared mostly in the rear -
view mirror. Looking ahead, buffers are running thin, fiscal policy needs
urgent consolidation, and downside risks (from energy and AI exube -
rance) loom large. Global headline inflation is expected to rise to 4.7%
in 2026, mainly due to energy and food, before easing back to 4.1% in
2027 (revised +0.2pp). Growth in advanced economies is revised up to
2.1% in 2026 (+0.4pp, driven by Germany), staying at 1.8% in 2027. Eu-

rozone growth is upgraded to 1.1% in 2026 (+0.2pp) and 1.2% in 2027.
US growth stays projected at 2.3% in 2026 and 2027 (+0.1pp). Germany
sees the largest G7 upgrade, to 1.3% in 2026 (+0.6pp) and 1.1% in 2027
(+0.1pp). France is the only major advanced economy downgraded, to
0.4% in 2026 (-0.2pp) and 0.7% in 2027 (-0.2pp). For more detailed
takeaways see Substack and this week’s EcoWeek editorial.
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Trade policy, competitiveness and budget agreements by year-end.

EU leaders asked the Commission to propose by year-end an instru -
ment allowing, in extreme cases, full closure of the single market to
a rival deemed to be attacking the EU economy. It is designed as a
“second-strike” deterrent against unfair practices and retaliation.
The Commission is also exploring extending the hybrid-vehicle safe -
guards approach agreed with China last week to other sectors such
as chemicals and machinery. On the "Made in Europe" label, vehicles

would need to be assembled in Europe with at least 70% of compo -
nent value (excluding batteries) sourced locally and with substantial
R&D in the EU. The UK would be included, provided London aligns with
EU trade defence measures. EU Council also called for decisive pro -
gress on the competitiveness agenda, with the single-market (inclu -
ding 26th regime) and simplification files to be wrapped up by year-end.
On the multi-annual financial framework, the objective remains a deal

by end-2026, so that legislation can be adopted in 2027.
EURO AREA
Moderate tightening does it. Multiple ECB Governing Council members,
speaking from the IMF Meetings in Bangkok, re-underwrote their
“moderate response” approach to the ongoing energy-driven infla -
tion surge. Accordingly, they signaled that additional rate action was
likely but not imminent, acknowledging that the rise in bond yields
was doing part of the job, and that there were no signs yet of second

round effects. Strengthening the case for moderation, the Eurozone in-
dustrial production fell in August for the second month running, by
0.5% m/m, pulled down by pharmaceutical output and motor vehicles.
That said, production in Netherlands surged to a new high (2.5% m/m
and 8.7% y/y), driven by AI machinery and equipment.
France
Business starts booming, insolvencies are stable, growth outlook is
downgraded. Business creations rose by 16% y/y in the 3 rd quarter

(+23% y/y in September), driven mainly by the digital sector and retail.
In parallel, business bankruptcies stabilised in the 3rd quarter according
to Altares, with a 0.5% increase y/y (compared with a 5.4% increase y/y
in Q2), and their impact on employment declined: 50,510 jobs at risk
in Q3, down 3% y/y (see our recent analysis). The Banque de France
halved its estimate for Q3 GDP to 0.1% q/q (same as Insee) due to
the manufacturing sector. Its monthly survey showed a sharp jump in

business uncertainty in September, driven by fuel prices and economic
deterioration (the most acute readings in industry and services since
April). The OAT-bund spread was rangebound (137-142).
Germany
Producer prices are rising. Wholesale prices rose 8.7% y/y (vs. 6.8% in
August) due to higher wholesale prices for energy products and inter -
mediate goods, particularly metals.
UNITED KINGDOM
Summer outperformance and kudos from IMF. Monthly GDP defied

expectations in August and posted a 0.1% m/m against a -0.1% consen-
sus. This marked a slowdown from July (0.4% m/m), but also a fourth
consecutive month of expansion. Industrial production underpinned
the advance: it accelerated to 0.8% m/m (consensus: -0.1%, prior: 0.3%)
thanks to pharmaceuticals. The IMF sees growth accelerating to 1.3%
in 2026 (+0.3 pp since July) and 1.2% in 2027. It also singled out the UK
among advanced economies for its efforts and progress in putting its

fiscal house in order.
UNITED STATES
Inflation heats up, manufacturing accelerates, but an October hike
is unlikely. CPI inflation exceeded expectations at 0.65% m/m in Sep -
tember (up 0.2pp), the fastest monthly pace since April. Accordingly,
the y/y rate climbed to 3.7% (up 0.3pp). The biggest upside surprise,
however, came from the core CPI: at 0.33% m/m (consensus: 0.2%, prior:
0.3%) and 2.6% y/y (consensus: 2.5%, prior: 2.4%); as services remained

sticky while goods accelerated to 0.3% m/m on autos and AI-related
items. Producer prices too rose well above expectations to 1.3% m/m
(consensus: 0.6%, prior: 0.5%) – the largest monthly increase since
March 2022 – and 6.3% y/y (consensus: 5.3%, prior: 5.6%). Industrial
production accelerated in September to 0.7% m/m (up 0.6pp) thanks
to utilities. Manufacturing returned to growth (0.3% m/m, up 0.5pp)
with gains broadening beyond the AI-related and aerospace sectors.

Retail sales excl. autos and gasoline, by contrast, slowed sharply to
0.2% m/m (down 0.9pp). During his speech at the IMF Meetings after
the hotter than expected inflation prints, Chair Warsh declined to hint
at an imminent rate hike. We continue to expect the next rate hike to
occur in December.
To watch this week:
- Developed markets Flash PMIs for October (Friday).
- Eurozone Germany September PPI, October ZEW (Tuesday), France Insee Business Climate (Thursday).

- UK August Employment data (Tuesday), September CPI and PPI (Wednesday), September Retail Sales (Friday).
- Japan September CPI, October PMI (Friday).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
JAPAN
Producer price inflation eases. Producer prices fell for a second month
in a row in September, down a further 0.5% m/m, while the yearly rate
slowed for the first time since February (-0.8pp), though it remains
firm at 6.8%. The IMF forecasts growth at 0.7% in both 2026 and 2027,
broadly unchanged. The yen weakened past 158 per dollar again after

reports that the BoJ is leaning against a back-to-back October rate hike.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Emerging markets
Asia’s outlook upgraded, other regions downgraded.
The IMF upgraded its Asia growth forecast to 4.8% in 2026 and 4.4% in
2027. The largest upgrades are for economies well integrated into the
AI supply chain, notably Taiwan (+5.5pp to 10.7%), South Korea (+0.7pp
to 3.3%), Singapore, Vietnam, Malaysia and to some extent Thailand.
India is forecast to grow 7% in both 2026 and 2027 (fiscal year basis),

up 0.6 and 0.3pp from July on robust domestic demand.
Central Europe’s GDP growth is projected at 2.1% in 2026, slightly
lower than April’s forecast. In 2027, it remains unchanged at 2.3%.
Growth paths are diverse within the region.
In the Middle East & North Africa, GDP is expected to contract by 2%
in 2026. GCC real GDP should fall by 4.8% with only Oman expected
to post positive growth (4.1%). A marked rebound is expected for 2027
(MENA: 7%; GCC: 9.1%).

The growth outlook for Latin America remains subdued, revised down
to 2.3% in 2026 (from 2.4%) and 2.5% in 2027 (from 2.7%). However, in
its "International Trade Outlook 2026" published on 15 October, the
Economic Commission for Latin America and the Caribbean (ECLAC) ex-
pects the region to experience a trade boom in 2026: goods exports are
projected to surge by 20% in value terms (11% from price increases and
9% from volume growth), with trade to Asia (excluding China) soaring

by 32%, while intraregional trade growth lags behind at 8%. Yet, these
export gains are concentrated in commodities and low-value-added
manufacturing, limiting their spillover to broader growth. Stronger
trade is thus providing only a modest boost to the economy.
China
Explosive export growth, subdued inflation and credit growth. In Sep-
tember, export value growth surged to 36.3% y/y from 25% in August,
driven by semiconductors, computer equipment and other AI goods, as

well as green technologies. Export prices surged, especially for semi -
conductors, while total export volumes remained robust. Import value
growth also accelerated to 31% y/y from 28.2% in August, boosted by
both rising volume and rapidly increasing prices. The trade surplus
hit a new monthly record of USD 134 bn in September. CPI inflation
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, mainly because of a 14.9% rise in
vehicle fuel prices. Food prices continued to fall (-1.7% y/y) and core

inflation was stable at 1%. Producer price inflation accelerated to 5%
(the highest since 2022) due to rising commodity prices. Growth in to-
tal domestic credit outstanding (“social financing”) continued to slow
down, reaching a record low of 7.1% y/y in September. Bank loans to
households declined (-2% y/y) for the seventh consecutive month.
In Q3 2026, real GDP growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y (1.2% q/q) from
4.3% y/y (0.9% q/q), primarily led by industrial production (which rose by

5.1% y/y on average in Q3 vs. 4.6% in Q2). On the demand side, exports
were the key driver while fixed-asset investment continued to contract.
Retail sales posted a small pickup in September but remained flat
in real terms in Q3 as a whole. With growth reaching 4.6% y/y year-to-
date, China needs only a 4.2% growth in Q4 to meet the official target of
4.5%-5% for this year – which seems easily feasible. The IMF trimmed
its growth forecast by 0.1pp to 4.5% in 2026 and 4% in 2027.

Indonesia
MSCI launched a consultation on reclassifying the country from Emer-
ging Market to Standalone Market status, with a final decision due by
end-June 2027, citing opaque shareholding structures and suspected
coordinating trading. Since January 2026, MSCI had frozen Index ad -
justments and threatened only a downgrade to Frontier market status
(like Pakistan), but market participants argued that a Frontier reclas -
sification would not properly reflect the extent of the market’s pro -

blems. Over the medium term, the risk is heavy foreign capital outflows
at a time when net FDI no longer covers the current account deficit.
This leaves Indonesia reliant on foreign portfolio inflows and the ru -
piah exposed to a reversal in sentiment. On the day of this announce -
ment, the Jakarta Composite Index fell by 11.6%, its steepest drop since
January 1998. Among Asian emerging markets, Indonesia has posted
the worst stock market performance since the beginning of the year

(-40.7%).
COMMODITIES
Iran/Oil. More of the same. Brent oil prices have remained broadly
stable this week (0.5% at 107$/b on Monday). Renewed Iranian at -
tacks on tankers in the Strait and intensification of Houthis’ attack over
the Saudi territory feed the geopolitical risk premium on barrel prices.
But IEA announcement of an accelerated 100 mb oil release (above
the March initial target of 400 mb) and reports that the US may lift
sanctions on the Russian and Iranian shadow fleets have contributed to

crude price stability. The spread with physical barrel (Dated Brent) has
declined (6.7$/b on Oct 16th). Trump administration’s various attempts
to lower diesel prices have had a limited impact for now. European gas
prices (TTF) recorded another weekly rise (4.7% w/w to 88.4 €/MWh,
the highest level since Dec 22nd, 2022) on the back of the rising number
of attacks on vessels in the Gulf and persisting disruption in the Strait.
However, EU gas inventories reached 74% of full capacities on October

16th (i.e., almost at the 80% month-end target), which should reduce
support for TTF prices going forward.
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels

Document 308: France’s unrest adds to an already toxic economic mix
